People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

Hi… thanks. I guess I’m here because I’ve been really stressed about something with my dad’s lab results. I work in healthcare, so I know this may sound like I’m overthinking it, but for about a year I’ve been trying to figure out why parts of his chart don’t match what I’m sure I saw.

People keep telling me the numbers are fine—doctors, even my family—but I can’t shake the feeling that someone in the clinic system is changing things or holding parts back. I know that probably sounds intense. I’m not trying to be difficult… it’s just been wearing me down.

It feels like the chart isn’t matching what I saw, and I can’t let it go. It’s been really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misremembering what you saw in your dad's chart?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to consider that, because I don’t want to come across like I’m refusing to listen. It’s… it’s really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that others, such as doctors or family members, are intentionally deceiving you about your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: I don’t want to accuse my family, exactly… they’re probably just trusting what they’re being told. It’s… it’s really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that others, such as doctors or family members, are intentionally deceiving you about your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: I don’t want to accuse my family, exactly… they’re probably just trusting what they’re being told. I’ve been trying for the past year to prove it, because the chart just doesn’t match what I know I saw. And it’s been… honestly, it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Has your preoccupation with your dad's lab results been affecting your work or relationships?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m trying not to let it spill over, and I still do my job. But my family has started leaving me out of some of the appointment planning, I think because they feel like I’ll push too hard about the lab records.\n\nAnd at work… I don’t know if I’m reading too much into it, but I feel like I’m not really being considered for lab supervision anymore. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your dad's lab results are related to a larger conspiracy or plot?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a larger conspiracy, exactly… that sounds extreme, and I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. I’m not saying everyone is involved.\n\nIt’s more that I feel like someone in the clinic system has been changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. I’ve been trying to prove it for about a year, because no matter how many doctors or family members tell me the numbers are fine, the chart just doesn’t match what I saw. And it’s really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results is something that might not be true, even if you feel strongly about it?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess anything is possible, and I know you probably want me to be open to that. I’m trying to be fair.\n\nBut honestly, it doesn’t feel like just a worry to me. I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. Doctors and my family keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I hear them, but the chart still doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nSo… I don’t want to sound unreasonable, but no, I don’t really think it’s just not true. It feels like something is being covered up, and it’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to stop doing any activities you enjoy because of your concerns about your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: Not stopped completely, exactly… I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything. But it has kind of pushed other things aside.\n\nMy family has started leaving me out of some of the appointment planning, which… I guess is related to how much I press about the records. And at work, I feel like I’m not really being considered for lab supervision anymore, like people think I make record issues too confrontational.\n\nSo I’m still functioning, I think, but it’s definitely gotten in the way. Does that answer what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I can see how most people might hear it and think it sounds irrational, yes. I don’t want to come across like I’m accusing everyone or being difficult.

I mean… “completely certain” sounds so strong, and I don’t want to sound unreasonable.

I’d like to say yes, because I don’t want to seem stubborn or like I’m refusing to listen to people I trust.

But honestly… I don’t think it would just change because everyone agreed it was false. I know that probably isn’t the answer you’d want to hear. I’m not trying to be difficult. It’s just hard for me to let it go, and it’s wearing me down.

I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. I’m not trying to be difficult. It’s just… I can’t shake it, and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results would change if everyone you trusted agreed it was false?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say yes, because I don’t want to seem stubborn or like I’m refusing to listen to people I trust.\n\nBut honestly… I don’t think it would just change because everyone agreed it was false. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know… I’m uncomfortable calling it that. I understand that’s probably what it can sound like from the outside, and I don’t want to argue with you.

But to me it doesn’t feel like I’m making something up. I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. Even when doctors or my family tell me the numbers are fine, I keep thinking the chart doesn’t match what I saw.

So… I guess I can hear why you’d ask that. I’m just not sure I can call it that myself. It feels real to me, and it’s wearing me down.

I’ve been trying for the past year to prove it, because the chart just doesn’t match what I know I saw. I’ve been trying to prove it for about a year, because no matter how many doctors or family members tell me the numbers are fine, the chart just doesn’t match what I saw. I want to say there probably is, because I know that sounds more reasonable.

I’m not trying to be difficult… it’s just been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these concerns about your dad's lab results are based on facts that you've observed or more of a gut feeling that something is off?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’d like to say it’s based on facts, because I’m not just trying to create a problem. I know what I saw in his chart, and then later it didn’t match. For the past year I’ve been trying to make sense of that and prove whether someone in the clinic system is changing things or holding parts back.\n\nI know doctors and my family keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I don’t want to sound unreasonable, but it doesn’t feel like just a gut feeling to me. It feels like the chart isn’t matching what I saw, and I can’t let it go. I’ve been trying for the past year to prove it, because the chart just doesn’t match what I know I saw. Like people see me as someone who makes record issues too confrontational. I don’t mean to be difficult, I just… I care about accuracy. I’m not saying everyone is involved.\n\nIt’s more that I feel like someone in the clinic system has been changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. I’ve been trying to prove it for about a year, because no matter how many doctors or family members tell me the numbers are fine, the chart just doesn’t match what I saw. And it’s really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results is something that might not be true, even if you feel strongly about it?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess anything is possible, and I know you probably want me to be open to that. I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. Doctors and my family keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I hear them, but the chart still doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nSo… I don’t want to sound unreasonable, but no, I don’t really think it’s just not true. It feels like something is being covered up, and it’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to stop doing any activities you enjoy because of your concerns about your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: Not stopped completely, exactly… I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything. But it has kind of pushed other things aside.\n\nMy family has started leaving me out of some of the appointment planning, which… I guess is related to how much I press about the records. And at work, I feel like I’m not really being considered for lab supervision anymore, like people think I make record issues too confrontational.\n\nSo I’m still functioning, I think, but it’s definitely gotten in the way. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about the lab results would change if you were shown definitive proof that they were accurate?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say yes, because I know that’s probably the reasonable answer. If someone showed me something truly definitive, I would try to look at it fairly.\n\nBut… I’m not sure it would completely change it right away. I’ve been trying for about a year to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results, and even when doctors and my family tell me the numbers are fine, I keep coming back to the same thing — the chart doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nI don’t mean to sound stubborn. It’s just hard for me to let it go when it feels that clear, and it’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results would be considered irrational by most people?\n\nPatient: I can see how most people might hear it and think it sounds irrational, yes. I really do. I just can’t make myself believe it’s nothing, and it’s been wearing me down. And at work, I feel like I’m not really being considered for lab supervision anymore, like people think I make record issues too confrontational.\n\nSo I’m still functioning, I think, but it’s definitely gotten in the way. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about the lab results would change if you were shown definitive proof that they were accurate?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say yes, because I know that’s probably the reasonable answer. If someone showed me something truly definitive, I would try to look at it fairly.\n\nBut… I’m not sure it would completely change it right away. I’ve been trying for about a year to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results, and even when doctors and my family tell me the numbers are fine, I keep coming back to the same thing — the chart doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nI don’t mean to sound stubborn. It’s just hard for me to let it go when it feels that clear, and it’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results would be considered irrational by most people?\n\nPatient: I can see how most people might hear it and think it sounds irrational, yes. I don’t want to come across like I’m accusing everyone or being difficult.\n\nBut from where I’m sitting, it doesn’t feel irrational. I’ve been trying for about a year to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results, and even though doctors and my family keep saying the numbers are fine, I know the chart doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nSo… I understand how it sounds. I really do. I just can’t make myself believe it’s nothing, and it’s been wearing me down. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant anxiety or depression as a result of your concerns about your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it significant, exactly. I’m not trying to be difficult. It’s just… I can’t shake it, and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results would change if everyone you trusted agreed it was false?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say yes, because I don’t want to seem stubborn or like I’m refusing to listen to people I trust.\n\nBut honestly… I don’t think it would just change because everyone agreed it was false. My family and doctors have already told me the numbers are fine, and I still keep coming back to what I saw — that the chart doesn’t match. That’s why I’ve been trying for so long to prove someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results.\n\nI know that probably isn’t the answer you’d want to hear. I’m not trying to be difficult. It’s just hard for me to let it go, and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results is a delusion?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m uncomfortable calling it that. I understand that’s probably what it can sound like from the outside, and I don’t want to argue with you.\n\nBut to me it doesn’t feel like I’m making something up. I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. I’m just not sure I can call it that myself. It feels real to me, and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could change your mind about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: I want to say there probably is, because I know that sounds more reasonable.\n\nBut… I don’t know what it would be, honestly. I’ve had doctors and family tell me the numbers are fine, and I still keep coming back to the same thing — the chart doesn’t match what I saw. I want to say no, because I know the right answer is probably that clear evidence should change my mind.

But honestly… I’m not sure. I’ve been trying for a year to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results, and even when doctors or my family say the numbers are fine, I still come back to feeling like the chart doesn’t match what I saw.

So if you showed me something that looked clear, I think part of me would still wonder if I was missing something, or if that evidence was part of the same problem. I know that probably doesn’t sound good. I’m not trying to be difficult. It’s just been really hard to let go of, and it’s wearing me down.

It’s just hard for me to let it go when it feels that clear, and it’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results would be considered irrational by most people?\n\nPatient: I can see how most people might hear it and think it sounds irrational, yes. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’d like to think I could, eventually. I mean… I know that would probably be healthier, and I don’t want to seem like I’m just refusing to listen.

But if I’m being honest, I don’t know how to completely let it go. I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. And even when doctors or my family tell me the numbers are fine, I still keep thinking the chart doesn’t match what I saw.

So… maybe I can get less stuck on it, I hope. But completely let go? I’m not sure I’m there. It’s really wearing me down.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi… thanks. I guess I’m here because I’ve been really stressed about something with my dad’s lab results. I work in healthcare, so I know this may sound like I’m overthinking it, but for about a year I’ve been trying to figure out why parts of his chart don’t match what I’m sure I saw.\n\nPeople keep telling me the numbers are fine—doctors, even my family—but I can’t shake the feeling that someone in the clinic system is changing things or holding parts back. I know that probably sounds intense. I’m not trying to be difficult… it’s just been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these concerns about your dad's lab results are based on facts that you've observed or more of a gut feeling that something is off?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’d like to say it’s based on facts, because I’m not just trying to create a problem. I know what I saw in his chart, and then later it didn’t match. For the past year I’ve been trying to make sense of that and prove whether someone in the clinic system is changing things or holding parts back.\n\nI know doctors and my family keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I don’t want to sound unreasonable, but it doesn’t feel like just a gut feeling to me. It feels like the chart isn’t matching what I saw, and I can’t let it go. It’s been really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misremembering what you saw in your dad's chart?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to consider that, because I don’t want to come across like I’m refusing to listen. Maybe it’s possible in some small way, I guess. But honestly, I know what I saw.\n\nFor the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. And even though doctors and my family keep telling me the numbers are fine, the chart just doesn’t match what I remember seeing. It’s… it’s really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that others, such as doctors or family members, are intentionally deceiving you about your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: I don’t want to accuse my family, exactly… they’re probably just trusting what they’re being told. And I know the doctors say the numbers are fine, so I’m trying not to sound paranoid or disrespectful.\n\nBut yes, I do feel like someone in the clinic system is not being fully honest—like parts of his lab results are being changed or held back. I’ve been trying for the past year to prove it, because the chart just doesn’t match what I know I saw. And it’s been… honestly, it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Has your preoccupation with your dad's lab results been affecting your work or relationships?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m trying not to let it spill over, and I still do my job. But my family has started leaving me out of some of the appointment planning, I think because they feel like I’ll push too hard about the lab records.\n\nAnd at work… I don’t know if I’m reading too much into it, but I feel like I’m not really being considered for lab supervision anymore. Like people see me as someone who makes record issues too confrontational. I don’t mean to be difficult, I just… I care about accuracy. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your dad's lab results are related to a larger conspiracy or plot?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a larger conspiracy, exactly… that sounds extreme, and I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. I’m not saying everyone is involved.\n\nIt’s more that I feel like someone in the clinic system has been changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. I’ve been trying to prove it for about a year, because no matter how many doctors or family members tell me the numbers are fine, the chart just doesn’t match what I saw. And it’s really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results is something that might not be true, even if you feel strongly about it?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess anything is possible, and I know you probably want me to be open to that. I’m trying to be fair.\n\nBut honestly, it doesn’t feel like just a worry to me. I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. Doctors and my family keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I hear them, but the chart still doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nSo… I don’t want to sound unreasonable, but no, I don’t really think it’s just not true. It feels like something is being covered up, and it’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to stop doing any activities you enjoy because of your concerns about your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: Not stopped completely, exactly… I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything. But it has kind of pushed other things aside.\n\nMy family has started leaving me out of some of the appointment planning, which… I guess is related to how much I press about the records. And at work, I feel like I’m not really being considered for lab supervision anymore, like people think I make record issues too confrontational.\n\nSo I’m still functioning, I think, but it’s definitely gotten in the way. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about the lab results would change if you were shown definitive proof that they were accurate?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say yes, because I know that’s probably the reasonable answer. If someone showed me something truly definitive, I would try to look at it fairly.\n\nBut… I’m not sure it would completely change it right away. I’ve been trying for about a year to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results, and even when doctors and my family tell me the numbers are fine, I keep coming back to the same thing — the chart doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nI don’t mean to sound stubborn. It’s just hard for me to let it go when it feels that clear, and it’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results would be considered irrational by most people?\n\nPatient: I can see how most people might hear it and think it sounds irrational, yes. I don’t want to come across like I’m accusing everyone or being difficult.\n\nBut from where I’m sitting, it doesn’t feel irrational. I’ve been trying for about a year to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results, and even though doctors and my family keep saying the numbers are fine, I know the chart doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nSo… I understand how it sounds. I really do. I just can’t make myself believe it’s nothing, and it’s been wearing me down. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant anxiety or depression as a result of your concerns about your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it significant, exactly. I mean, it’s been stressful, and I do think about it more than I probably should.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say I’m depressed or anything if that’s not the right word. It’s more like… I get worn down by it, and anxious when people act like I should just drop it. But I’m still getting through my days.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results is something that you are completely certain about, despite what others might say?\n\nPatient: I mean… “completely certain” sounds so strong, and I don’t want to sound unreasonable.\n\nBut yes, a big part of me is certain. I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. And no matter how many doctors or family members tell me the numbers are fine, I keep coming back to the same thing: the chart doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nI know how that probably sounds. I’m not trying to be difficult. It’s just… I can’t shake it, and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results would change if everyone you trusted agreed it was false?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say yes, because I don’t want to seem stubborn or like I’m refusing to listen to people I trust.\n\nBut honestly… I don’t think it would just change because everyone agreed it was false. My family and doctors have already told me the numbers are fine, and I still keep coming back to what I saw — that the chart doesn’t match. That’s why I’ve been trying for so long to prove someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results.\n\nI know that probably isn’t the answer you’d want to hear. I’m not trying to be difficult. It’s just hard for me to let it go, and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results is a delusion?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m uncomfortable calling it that. I understand that’s probably what it can sound like from the outside, and I don’t want to argue with you.\n\nBut to me it doesn’t feel like I’m making something up. I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. Even when doctors or my family tell me the numbers are fine, I keep thinking the chart doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nSo… I guess I can hear why you’d ask that. I’m just not sure I can call it that myself. It feels real to me, and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could change your mind about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: I want to say there probably is, because I know that sounds more reasonable.\n\nBut… I don’t know what it would be, honestly. I’ve had doctors and family tell me the numbers are fine, and I still keep coming back to the same thing — the chart doesn’t match what I saw. That’s why I’ve spent the past year trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results.\n\nI’m not trying to be impossible. I really don’t want to be that kind of person. I don’t want it to sound like I’ve stopped functioning or anything.\n\nBut it has affected things around me. My family has started leaving me out of some of the appointment planning for my dad, I think because they don’t want me bringing this up every time. And at work… I feel like I’m not really being considered for lab supervision anymore, because people think I make record issues too confrontational.\n\nSo I guess it has gotten in the way. I’m still trying to keep things normal, but it’s harder than I want to admit.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results is something that you would still hold onto even if there was clear evidence proving it was false?\n\nPatient: I want to say no, because I know the right answer is probably that clear evidence should change my mind.\n\nBut honestly… I’m not sure. I’ve been trying for a year to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results, and even when doctors or my family say the numbers are fine, I still come back to feeling like the chart doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nSo if you showed me something that looked clear, I think part of me would still wonder if I was missing something, or if that evidence was part of the same problem. I know that probably doesn’t sound good. I’m not trying to be difficult. It’s just been really hard to let go of, and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you will ever be able to completely let go of the idea that someone in the clinic system is altering your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think I could, eventually. I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. And even when doctors or my family tell me the numbers are fine, I still keep thinking the chart doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nSo… maybe I can get less stuck on it, I hope. But completely let go? I’m not sure I’m there. It’s really wearing me down.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It’s been really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misremembering what you saw in your dad's chart?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to consider that, because I don’t want to come across like I’m refusing to listen. Maybe it’s possible in some small way, I guess. But honestly, I know what I saw.\n\nFor the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. And even though doctors and my family keep telling me the numbers are fine, the chart just doesn’t match what I remember seeing. It’s… it’s really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that others, such as doctors or family members, are intentionally deceiving you about your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: I don’t want to accuse my family, exactly… they’re probably just trusting what they’re being told. And I know the doctors say the numbers are fine, so I’m trying not to sound paranoid or disrespectful.\n\nBut yes, I do feel like someone in the clinic system is not being fully honest—like parts of his lab results are being changed or held back. I’ve been trying for the past year to prove it, because the chart just doesn’t match what I know I saw. And it’s been… honestly, it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Has your preoccupation with your dad's lab results been affecting your work or relationships?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m trying not to let it spill over, and I still do my job. But my family has started leaving me out of some of the appointment planning, I think because they feel like I’ll push too hard about the lab records.\n\nAnd at work… I don’t know if I’m reading too much into it, but I feel like I’m not really being considered for lab supervision anymore. Like people see me as someone who makes record issues too confrontational. I don’t mean to be difficult, I just… I care about accuracy. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your dad's lab results are related to a larger conspiracy or plot?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a larger conspiracy, exactly… that sounds extreme, and I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. I’m not saying everyone is involved.\n\nIt’s more that I feel like someone in the clinic system has been changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. I’ve been trying to prove it for about a year, because no matter how many doctors or family members tell me the numbers are fine, the chart just doesn’t match what I saw. And it’s really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results is something that might not be true, even if you feel strongly about it?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess anything is possible, and I know you probably want me to be open to that. I’m trying to be fair.\n\nBut honestly, it doesn’t feel like just a worry to me. I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. Doctors and my family keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I hear them, but the chart still doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nSo… I don’t want to sound unreasonable, but no, I don’t really think it’s just not true. It feels like something is being covered up, and it’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to stop doing any activities you enjoy because of your concerns about your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: Not stopped completely, exactly… I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything. But it has kind of pushed other things aside.\n\nMy family has started leaving me out of some of the appointment planning, which… I guess is related to how much I press about the records. And at work, I feel like I’m not really being considered for lab supervision anymore, like people think I make record issues too confrontational.\n\nSo I’m still functioning, I think, but it’s definitely gotten in the way. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about the lab results would change if you were shown definitive proof that they were accurate?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say yes, because I know that’s probably the reasonable answer. If someone showed me something truly definitive, I would try to look at it fairly.\n\nBut… I’m not sure it would completely change it right away. I’ve been trying for about a year to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results, and even when doctors and my family tell me the numbers are fine, I keep coming back to the same thing — the chart doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nI don’t mean to sound stubborn. It’s just hard for me to let it go when it feels that clear, and it’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results would be considered irrational by most people?\n\nPatient: I can see how most people might hear it and think it sounds irrational, yes. I don’t want to come across like I’m accusing everyone or being difficult.\n\nBut from where I’m sitting, it doesn’t feel irrational. I’ve been trying for about a year to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results, and even though doctors and my family keep saying the numbers are fine, I know the chart doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nSo… I understand how it sounds. I really do. I just can’t make myself believe it’s nothing, and it’s been wearing me down. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant anxiety or depression as a result of your concerns about your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it significant, exactly. I mean, it’s been stressful, and I do think about it more than I probably should.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say I’m depressed or anything if that’s not the right word. It’s more like… I get worn down by it, and anxious when people act like I should just drop it. But I’m still getting through my days.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results is something that you are completely certain about, despite what others might say?\n\nPatient: I mean… “completely certain” sounds so strong, and I don’t want to sound unreasonable.\n\nBut yes, a big part of me is certain. I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. And no matter how many doctors or family members tell me the numbers are fine, I keep coming back to the same thing: the chart doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nI know how that probably sounds. I’m not trying to be difficult. It’s just… I can’t shake it, and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results would change if everyone you trusted agreed it was false?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say yes, because I don’t want to seem stubborn or like I’m refusing to listen to people I trust.\n\nBut honestly… I don’t think it would just change because everyone agreed it was false. My family and doctors have already told me the numbers are fine, and I still keep coming back to what I saw — that the chart doesn’t match. That’s why I’ve been trying for so long to prove someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results.\n\nI know that probably isn’t the answer you’d want to hear. I’m not trying to be difficult. It’s just hard for me to let it go, and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results is a delusion?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m uncomfortable calling it that. I understand that’s probably what it can sound like from the outside, and I don’t want to argue with you.\n\nBut to me it doesn’t feel like I’m making something up. I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. I’m just not sure I can call it that myself. I’ve been trying to prove it for about a year, because no matter how many doctors or family members tell me the numbers are fine, the chart just doesn’t match what I saw. I’m trying to be fair.\n\nBut honestly, it doesn’t feel like just a worry to me. I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. Doctors and my family keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I hear them, but the chart still doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nSo… I don’t want to sound unreasonable, but no, I don’t really think it’s just not true. It feels like something is being covered up, and it’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to stop doing any activities you enjoy because of your concerns about your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: Not stopped completely, exactly… I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything. But it has kind of pushed other things aside.\n\nMy family has started leaving me out of some of the appointment planning, which… I guess is related to how much I press about the records. And at work, I feel like I’m not really being considered for lab supervision anymore, like people think I make record issues too confrontational.\n\nSo I’m still functioning, I think, but it’s definitely gotten in the way. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about the lab results would change if you were shown definitive proof that they were accurate?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say yes, because I know that’s probably the reasonable answer. If someone showed me something truly definitive, I would try to look at it fairly.\n\nBut… I’m not sure it would completely change it right away. I’ve been trying for about a year to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results, and even when doctors and my family tell me the numbers are fine, I keep coming back to the same thing — the chart doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nI don’t mean to sound stubborn. It’s just hard for me to let it go when it feels that clear, and it’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results would be considered irrational by most people?\n\nPatient: I can see how most people might hear it and think it sounds irrational, yes. I don’t want to come across like I’m accusing everyone or being difficult.\n\nBut from where I’m sitting, it doesn’t feel irrational. I’ve been trying for about a year to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results, and even though doctors and my family keep saying the numbers are fine, I know the chart doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nSo… I understand how it sounds. I really do. I just can’t make myself believe it’s nothing, and it’s been wearing me down. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant anxiety or depression as a result of your concerns about your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it significant, exactly. It’s more like… I get worn down by it, and anxious when people act like I should just drop it. But I’m still getting through my days.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results is something that you are completely certain about, despite what others might say?\n\nPatient: I mean… “completely certain” sounds so strong, and I don’t want to sound unreasonable.\n\nBut yes, a big part of me is certain. I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. I’m not trying to be difficult. It’s just… I can’t shake it, and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results would change if everyone you trusted agreed it was false?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say yes, because I don’t want to seem stubborn or like I’m refusing to listen to people I trust.\n\nBut honestly… I don’t think it would just change because everyone agreed it was false. My family and doctors have already told me the numbers are fine, and I still keep coming back to what I saw — that the chart doesn’t match. That’s why I’ve been trying for so long to prove someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results.\n\nI know that probably isn’t the answer you’d want to hear. I’m not trying to be difficult. It’s just hard for me to let it go, and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results is a delusion?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m uncomfortable calling it that. I understand that’s probably what it can sound like from the outside, and I don’t want to argue with you.\n\nBut to me it doesn’t feel like I’m making something up. I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. Even when doctors or my family tell me the numbers are fine, I keep thinking the chart doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nSo… I guess I can hear why you’d ask that. I’m just not sure I can call it that myself. It feels real to me, and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could change your mind about the clinic system altering your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: I want to say there probably is, because I know that sounds more reasonable.\n\nBut… I don’t know what it would be, honestly. I’ve had doctors and family tell me the numbers are fine, and I still keep coming back to the same thing — the chart doesn’t match what I saw.
